r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a directed graph with N vertices and M edges. The vertices are numbered 1,2,\dots,N. Edge j (j=1,2,\dots,M) goes from vertex u_j to vertex v_j. It is guaranteed that vertex N is reachable from vertex 1.
Initially, all edges have weight 0. We choose exactly K out of the M edges and change their weights to 1. Find the maximum possible value of the shortest distance from vertex 1 to vertex N in the resulting graph.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M K
u_1 v_1
u_2 v_2
\vdots
u_M v_M

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 30
- 1 \leq K \leq M \leq 100
- 1 \leq u_j, v_j \leq N
- u_j \neq v_j
- In the given graph, vertex N is reachable from vertex 1.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 3 2
1 2
2 3
1 3

Sample Output 1

1

By choosing edges 1,3, the shortest distance from vertex 1 to vertex 3 becomes 1. There is no way to make the shortest distance 2 or greater, so the answer is 1.

Sample Input 2

4 4 3
1 2
1 3
3 2
2 4

Sample Output 2

2

By choosing edges 1,2,4, the shortest distance from vertex 1 to vertex 4 becomes 2. There is no way to make the shortest distance 3 or greater, so the answer is 2.

Sample Input 3

2 2 1
1 2
1 2

Sample Output 3

0

Note that there may be multi-edges.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's formalize H_t construction in code:

   For each original edge (u,v):
       add_edge(id(u,i), id(v,i), 1)  # cut capacity if u in A_i and v not.
- For each layer i in 0..t-2:
   For each vertex v:
       add_edge(id(v,i), id(v,i+1), INF)  # monotonicity A_i⊆A_{i+1}
   For each original edge (u,v):
       add_edge(id(u,i), id(v,i+1), INF)  # edge constraint no jump >1

Let's write Dinic:

class Dinic:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n
        self.ad=[[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr,to,cap):
        self.ad[fr].append([to, cap, len(self.ad[to])])
        self.ad[to].append([fr, 0, len(self.ad[fr])-1])
But using list of lists with mutable reverse indices can be tricky. Use Edge class with to, rev, cap. For performance, fine.

Implement max_flow(s,t,limit):
    flow=0
    INF=10**18
    while flow<limit:
        level=[-1]*n; BFS from s; if t unreachable break
        it=[0]*n
        while flow<limit:
            f=dfs(s, limit-flow)
            if f==0: break
            flow += f
    return flow

But if there are many parallel infinite edges, DFS may push limit in one path; okay.

Implementation details:

def feasible(t):
    if t == 0: return True
    V = N * t
    dinic = Dinic(V)
    def id(v, layer): return layer * N + v  # v 0-index
    s = id(0, 0)
    tgt = id(N-1, t-1)
    INF = K + 1  # since only compare <=K
    # capacity edges
    for layer in range(t):
        for u,v in edges:
            dinic.add_edge(id(u,layer), id(v,layer), 1)
    # infinite edges
    for layer in range(t-1):
        for v in range(N):
            dinic.add_edge(id(v,layer), id(v,layer+1), INF)
        for u,v in edges:
            dinic.add_edge(id(u,layer), id(v,layer+1), INF)
    flow = dinic.max_flow(s, tgt, K+1)
    return flow <= K

Need ensure if t=1, range(t-1)=range(0) no infinite. Good.

But what if maxflow with limit K+1 and INF=K+1: In Dinic, if there is a path source->...->sink with one INF edge and no capacity edges, DFS pushes K+1. Good. If there is a path with two INF edges, also pushes K+1. Good. If there is a finite cut of capacity K+1, maxflow reaches K+1. Good.

Implement Edge as lists for speed:

class Edge:
    __slots__ = ('to','rev','cap')
    def __init__(self,to,rev,cap):
        self.to=to; self.rev=rev; self.cap=cap

add_edge:
self.g[fr].append(Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap))
self.g[to].append(Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr])-1, 0))

dfs:
def dfs(v,t,f):
    if v==t: return f
    for i in range(it[v], len(g[v])):
        e = g[v][i]
        if e.cap>0 and level[v]<level[e.to]:
            d=dfs(e.to,t,min(f,e.cap))
            if d:
                e.cap-=d
                g[e.to][e.rev].cap += d
                return d
        it[v]+=1
    return 0

Need update it correctly. Common:

while it[v] < len(g[v]):
    e = g[v][it[v]]
    if e.cap > 0 and level[v] < level[e.to]: # or level[e.to] == level[v]+1
        d=dfs(...)
        if d>0: ...
    it[v]+=1
return 0

Use level[e.to] == level[v]+1 for Dinic. Good.

BFS:
level=[-1]*n; q=deque([s]); level[s]=0
while q: ...
 if e.cap>0 and level[e.to]<0: level[e.to]=level[v]+1

max_flow:
flow=0
while flow<limit:
    bfs
    if level[t]<0: break
    it=[0]*n
    while flow<limit:
        f=dfs(s,t,limit-flow)
        if f==0: break
        flow+=f
return flow

Implementation plan:

import sys
from collections import deque

class Dinic:
    ...
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N,M,K=map(int,input().split())
    edges=[]
    adj=[[] for _ in range(N)]
    for _ in range(M):
        u,v=map(int,input().split()); u-=1; v-=1
        edges.append((u,v))
        adj[u].append(v)
    # BFS
    dist=[-1]*N; dist[0]=0; q=deque([0])
    while q:
        u=q.popleft()
        for v in adj[u]:
            if dist[v]==-1:
                dist[v]=dist[u]+1
                q.append(v)
    hi = min(K, dist[N-1])  # dist guaranteed
    lo=0
    def feasible(t):
        if t==0: return True
        V=N*t
        dinic=Dinic(V)
        def node(v, layer): return layer*N + v
        s=node(0,0)
        sink=node(N-1,t-1)
        INF=K+1
        # capacity edges
        for layer in range(t):
            base=layer*N
            for u,v in edges:
                dinic.add_edge(base+u, base+v, 1)
        # infinite edges
        for layer in range(t-1):
            base=layer*N
            next_base=(layer+1)*N
            for v in range(N):
                dinic.add_edge(base+v, next_base+v, INF)
            for u,v in edges:
                dinic.add_edge(base+u, next_base+v, INF)
        return dinic.max_flow(s, sink, K+1) <= K
    while lo<hi:
        mid=(lo+hi+1)//2
        if feasible(mid):
            lo=mid
        else:
            hi=mid-1
    print(lo)

Let's write Dinic carefully:

class Edge:
    __slots__ = ("to","rev","cap")
    def __init__(self,to,rev,cap):
        self.to=to
        self.rev=rev
        self.cap=cap

class Dinic:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n
        self.g=[[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self,fr,to,cap):
        f=Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
        r=Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
        self.g[fr].append(f)
        self.g[to].append(r)
    def bfs(self,s,t):
        self.level=[-1]*self.n
        q=deque([s])
        self.level[s]=0
        while q:
            v=q.popleft()
            for e in self.g[v]:
                if e.cap>0 and self.level[e.to]<0:
                    self.level[e.to]=self.level[v]+1
                    if e.to==t:
                        # can continue? We can early return? For Dinic, need full level graph? We can stop BFS when t reached? Usually can stop early? It may still work? Better not early return to build full levels? Dinic correctness doesn't require full BFS? It requires levels up to t; edges beyond t not needed. Early return okay but simpler no early.
                    q.append(e.to)
        return self.level[t]>=0
    def dfs(self,v,t,f):
        if v==t:
            return f
        for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.g[v])):
            self.it[v]=i
            e=self.g[v][i]
            if e.cap>0 and self.level[v]+1==self.level[e.to]:
                ret=self.dfs(e.to,t,min(f,e.cap))
                if ret:
                    e.cap-=ret
                    self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                    return ret
            self.it[v]+=1
        return 0

Need be careful with `self.it[v]=i` and increment. Common pattern:

while self.it[v] < len(self.g[v]):
    e = self.g[v][self.it[v]]
    if e.cap > 0 and self.level[v] < self.level[e.to]:
        d = self.dfs(e.to,t,min(f,e.cap))
        if d > 0:
            e.cap -= d
            self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += d
            return d
    self.it[v] += 1
return 0

Using `level[v] < level[e.to]` or `== level[v]+1`; in Dinic level graph should be +1. Use ==.

But note if we use `for i in range(self.it[v], len(...))` and set `self.it[v]=i`, then after failed edge, increment. However if recursive dfs returns 0, we need increment. The while pattern is safer.

Implement dfs:

def dfs(self, v, t, f):
    if v == t:
        return f
    gv = self.g[v]
    while self.it[v] < len(gv):
        e = gv[self.it[v]]
        if e.cap > 0 and self.level[e.to] == self.level[v] + 1:
            ret = self.dfs(e.to, t, min(f, e.cap))
            if ret > 0:
                e.cap -= ret
                self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                return ret
        self.it[v] += 1
    return 0

Potential issue: `gv = self.g[v]` and then `self.it[v]` increments; okay. But if recursive call modifies self.g[v]? It may modify reverse edges of e, which are in g[e.to], not g[v], except if e.to==v? no self-loops absent but could have reverse edge to v? Not in same list. So gv remains valid. Good.

max_flow:

def max_flow(self,s,t,limit):
    flow=0
    INF=10**18
    while flow<limit:
        if not self.bfs(s,t):
            break
        self.it=[0]*self.n
        while flow<limit:
            f=self.dfs(s,t,limit-flow)
            if f==0:
                break
            flow += f
    return flow

Any finite s-t cut (S,T) with source in S, sink in T. Define A_i={v | (v,i)∈S}. Because infinite edges (v,i)->(v,i+1) not cut, A_i⊆A_{i+1}. Because infinite edges (u,i)->(v,i+1) not cut, if u∈A_i then v∈A_{i+1}. Source/sink give s∈A0, N∉A_{t-1}. Define ℓ(v)=min{i | v∈A_i}, or t if none. Then ℓ(s)=0, ℓ(N)=t. Edge constraint: for any e=(u,v), let a=ℓ(u). If a≤t-2, then u∈A_a, so v∈A_{a+1}, hence ℓ(v)≤a+1. If a=t-1, ℓ(v)≤t=a+1. Thus ℓ(v)≤ℓ(u)+1. Cut capacity = ∑_{i=0}^{t-1} |δ^+(A_i)|. For an edge e=(u,v), it contributes to cut capacity for each i with u∈A_i, v∉A_i. This happens iff ℓ(u)≤i<ℓ(v). Since ℓ(v)≤ℓ(u)+1, the number of such i is 1 if ℓ(v)=ℓ(u)+1, else 0. Thus cut capacity = number of edges with ℓ(v)>ℓ(u) = upward edges. Good.

Conversely, given labels ℓ with ℓ(s)=0,ℓ(N)=t, ℓ(v)≤ℓ(u)+1, define A_i={v|ℓ(v)≤i} for i=0..t-1. Then A_i nested, s∈A0, N∉A_{t-1}. Then min upward edges = min selected edges:
- Given labels, select all upward edges. For any path P, sum over edges (ℓ(v)-ℓ(u)) = t. For selected upward edge, ℓ(v)-ℓ(u)=1; for unselected non-upward, ≤0; but what about selected non-upward?    if e∈S, d(v)≤d(u)+1 (with ∞ conventions), so ℓ(v)≤ℓ(u)+1.

Thus if ℓ(v)>ℓ(u), e must be in S. Hence upward edges count≤|S|. So min upward edges≤min selected. Combined equality.

r.rev = len(self.g[fr])

Let's maybe write code now mentally:

import sys
from collections import deque

sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000)

class Edge:
    __slots__ = ('to','rev','cap')
    def __init__(self,to,rev,cap):
        self.to=to
        self.rev=rev
        self.cap=cap

class Dinic:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n
        self.g=[[] for _ in range(n)]
        self.level=[0]*n
        self.it=[0]*n
    def add_edge(self,fr,to,cap):
        f=Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
        r=Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
        self.g[fr].append(f)
        self.g[to].append(r)
    def bfs(self,s,t):
        level=[-1]*self.n
        q=deque([s])
        level[s]=0
        g=self.g
        while q:
            v=q.popleft()
            nv=level[v]+1
            for e in g[v]:
                if e.cap>0 and level[e.to]<0:
                    level[e.to]=nv
                    q.append(e.to)
        self.level=level
        return level[t]>=0
    def dfs(self,v,t,f):
        if v==t:
            return f
        g=self.g
        level=self.level
        it=self.it
        while it[v] < len(g[v]):
            e=g[v][it[v]]
            if e.cap>0 and level[e.to] == level[v]+1:
                ret=self.dfs(e.to,t,min(f,e.cap))
                if ret:
                    e.cap-=ret
                    g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                    return ret
            it[v]+=1
        return 0
    def max_flow(self,s,t,limit):
        flow=0
        while flow<limit:
            if not self.bfs(s,t):
                break
            self.it=[0]*self.n
            while flow<limit:
                f=self.dfs(s,t,limit-flow)
                if f==0:
                    break
                flow+=f
        return flow

Potential issue: In dfs, `g=self.g` and `it=self.it` local; recursive call uses self.dfs, which will create new locals. Good. But after recursive call returns, `it[v]` may have been modified by deeper? No, deeper modifies its own indices, not parent's `it[v]` except if same v? Recursion goes forward, not back to v. So okay.

Potential issue: In bfs, setting `self.level=level` after BFS; dfs uses self.level. Good.

Potential issue: In dfs, if `ret` is 0, we increment it[v]. If `ret` positive, we return without incrementing; next dfs call may use same edge if residual remains. Good.

Potential issue: If `e.cap` becomes 0 after ret, next dfs will skip due cap>0 false and increment. Good.

Now, main:

def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N,M,K=map(int,input().split())
    edges=[]
    adj=[[] for _ in range(N)]
    for _ in range(M):
        u,v=map(int,input().split())
        u-=1; v-=1
        edges.append((u,v))
        adj[u].append(v)
    dist=[-1]*N
    dist[0]=0
    q=deque([0])
    while q:
        u=q.popleft()
        for v in adj[u]:
            if dist[v]==-1:
                dist[v]=dist[u]+1
                q.append(v)
    hi=min(K, dist[N-1])
    lo=0
    def feasible(t):
        if t==0:
            return True
        V=N*t
        dinic=Dinic(V)
        def node(v, layer):
            return layer*N + v
        s=node(0,0)
        sink=node(N-1,t-1)
        INF=K+1
        # capacity edges
        for layer in range(t):
            base=layer*N
            for u,v in edges:
                dinic.add_edge(base+u, base+v, 1)
        # infinite edges
        for layer in range(t-1):
            base=layer*N
            nbase=(layer+1)*N
            for v in range(N):
                dinic.add_edge(base+v, nbase+v, INF)
            for u,v in edges:
                dinic.add_edge(base+u, nbase+v, INF)
        return dinic.max_flow(s, sink, K+1) <= K
    while lo<hi:
        mid=(lo+hi+1)//2
        if feasible(mid):
            lo=mid
        else:
            hi=mid-1
    print(lo)

If actual mincut>2, maxflow would reach limit2 and stop, returning2. This would falsely return true! Wait important! We set limit = K+1. If actual mincut > K, maxflow with limit K+1 will push K+1, not K. Because limit is K+1. So if K=2, limit=3. If actual mincut>2, maxflow reaches 3 and stops, returns3, false. Good. If we had set limit=K, then false positives. But we use K+1. Good.

Let's double-check: max_flow(s,t,limit) stops when flow >= limit. If actual maxflow > limit, returns limit. For K=2, limit=3. If actual mincut=3, returns3 false. If actual mincut=4, returns3 false. If actual mincut=2, returns2 true. So no false positive. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
? The final should be code only. I'll provide the final code.

Need ensure no accidental "oup" text. Final code block.

Let's produce final.</think>```python
import sys
from collections import deque

sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000)


class Edge:
    __slots__ = ("to", "rev", "cap")

    def __init__(self, to, rev, cap):
        self.to = to
        self.rev = rev
        self.cap = cap


class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.g = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        self.level = [0] * n
        self.it = [0] * n

    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        fwd = Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
        rev = Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
        self.g[fr].append(fwd)
        self.g[to].append(rev)

    def bfs(self, s, t):
        self.level = [-1] * self.n
        q = deque([s])
        self.level[s] = 0
        while q:
            v = q.popleft()
            for e in self.g[v]:
                if e.cap > 0 and self.level[e.to] < 0:
                    self.level[e.to] = self.level[v] + 1
                    q.append(e.to)
        return self.level[t] >= 0

    def dfs(self, v, t, f):
        if v == t:
            return f
        while self.it[v] < len(self.g[v]):
            e = self.g[v][self.it[v]]
            if e.cap > 0 and self.level[e.to] == self.level[v] + 1:
                ret = self.dfs(e.to, t, min(f, e.cap))
                if ret > 0:
                    e.cap -= ret
                    self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                    return ret
            self.it[v] += 1
        return 0

    def max_flow(self, s, t, limit):
        flow = 0
        while flow < limit:
            if not self.bfs(s, t):
                break
            self.it = [0] * self.n
            while flow < limit:
                f = self.dfs(s, t, limit - flow)
                if f == 0:
                    break
                flow += f
        return flow


def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline

    N, M, K = map(int, input().split())
    edges = []
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]

    for _ in range(M):
        u, v = map(int, input().split())
        u -= 1
        v -= 1
        edges.append((u, v))
        adj[u].append(v)

    # Original shortest path length gives an upper bound.
    dist = [-1] * N
    dist[0] = 0
    q = deque([0])
    while q:
        u = q.popleft()
        for v in adj[u]:
            if dist[v] == -1:
                dist[v] = dist[u] + 1
                q.append(v)

    hi = min(K, dist[N - 1])
    lo = 0

    def feasible(t):
        if t == 0:
            return True

        V = N * t
        dinic = Dinic(V)

        def node(v, layer):
            return layer * N + v

        s = node(0, 0)
        sink = node(N - 1, t - 1)
        INF = K + 1

        # Capacity edges: choosing this original edge at this layer.
        for layer in range(t):
            base = layer * N
            for u, v in edges:
                dinic.add_edge(base + u, base + v, 1)

        # Infinite edges: enforce monotonicity and label increase at most 1.
        for layer in range(t - 1):
            base = layer * N
            nbase = (layer + 1) * N

            for v in range(N):
                dinic.add_edge(base + v, nbase + v, INF)

            for u, v in edges:
                dinic.add_edge(base + u, nbase + v, INF)

        return dinic.max_flow(s, sink, K + 1) <= K

    while lo < hi:
        mid = (lo + hi + 1) // 2
        if feasible(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid - 1

    print(lo)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
